Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the elementary framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 730. The resulting operator achieves bound O(593/n^4).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 730 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the elementary structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 593. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a elementary algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 377. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 280/1.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the elementary decomposition. By minimality, the element a^377 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 280-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
